The optimization process's characteristics—such as the large initial objective range, sporadic improvements in \( f_{\text{min}} \), fluctuating model uncertainty (outputscale), and the need for alternating exploration (UCB, TS) and exploitation (EI) strategies—suggest the objective function is a **high-dimensional, multimodal function with a narrow global minimum surrounded by noisy or rugged regions**. 
A plausible candidate is the **Hartmann function** (specifically a 5-dimensional variant), commonly used in Bayesian Optimization benchmarks. It features:  
- A single global minimum in a complex, multimodal landscape.  
- High sensitivity to parameter changes, requiring careful exploration.  
- Regions of high uncertainty (explaining the model's variable outputscale).  
- The need for balancing exploration (e.g., UCB/TS in tight clusters) and exploitation (EI near promising regions).  
The rare \( f_{\text{min}} \) improvements and persistent model uncertainty align with the challenges of navigating such a function.
